Registration lifecycle or scoped restrictions may invalidate reuse from the first changed schema token."
- `### Results` → *What the model sees* quotes the exact acknowledgement strings and both empty-listing sentinels and states that invalid input returns an `isError` result quoting the zod issue path → *Token effect*: "Results remain in history until compaction; listings are bounded by `maxListedRules` and `maxListedRuns` after any model-supplied `limit`." → *KV Cache effect*: "Append-only; newly visible content follows the reusable request prefix and does not invalidate existing KV-cache entries."

Then `## Known Limitations and Deferred Work` (`:52-56`) with three bolded gaps (edit-by-replacement, no pagination beyond caps, no delete tool + why). Preceding sections: a one-paragraph intro naming the seam and stating what it does *not* do ("registers no prompt section and appends no session events of its own"), a `## Tools` bullet per tool with its signature and result grammar, and a `## Config` table (`key | default | meaning`). Canonical format spec: `docs/cookbook/adding-a-package.md:73-107`.

**Translation pairing**: `README.zh.md` plus `README.i18n.yaml` recording both git blob hashes; re-record with `pnpm run verify-translation-pairing --write &lt;path&gt;`. Full doc-sync gate list at `scripts/run-gates.ts:651-685`.

## 2. Runtime flow of a tool call

**Registry → prompt.** `ToolRuntime`'s constructor wires the assembly once: `packages/core/tools/src/index.ts:832` — `ctx.systemPrompt.tools(context =&gt; this.wireSchemas(context.scope))`. Schemas flow in automatically; nothing per-tool is registered with the prompt. `schemas(scope?)` whitelists only name/description/parameters (`:251`). Under `mode: code`/`both` the constructor also adds `tools:code-only` and `tools:sdk` sections (`:833-836`), so a new tool is reachable as `await tools.&lt;name&gt;(args)` for free (`docs/cookbook/adding-a-tool.md:61-65`).

**Execution waterfall.** `ctx.tools.execute(exec)` → lossless-snapshot + freeze `arguments`, mint an opaque `exec.token` → `tools/pre-execute` waterfall (`:1476`) → `ask` resolution through the approval seam (`:1679-1726`) → monotonic `ctx.tools.guard()` denials → `tools/execute` around-wrapper → the definition's `execute(args, exec)` → `tools/post-execute` → definition-owned `finalizeContent` → observe-only `tools/result`. The generated diagram is `docs/tool-execution-pipeline.md`. The durable `tool/call` event is logged *before* execution and `tool/result` *after*; `tools/result` (live) and `tool/result` (durable) are distinct (`packages/core/tools/README.md:37-39`).

**Approvals / permission.** The registry consumes the approval seam **opportunistically** — `ctx.get('approval')`, no static inject (`packages/core/tools/README.md:31`, code at `:1693-1697`): absent approval turns `ask` into deny with `tool "&lt;name&gt;" requires approval (not yet supported)`; an `ask` with no `exec.agent` denies at `:1702`. What *decides* a tool needs approval is a `tools/pre-execute` listener returning `{ kind: 'ask' }` — the only shipped producers are the hook bridges (`packages/hooks/hooks-claude-code/src/index.ts:242`, `packages/hooks/hooks-codex/src/index.ts:225`) and the sandbox escalation closure (`packages/sandbox/sandbox/src/escalation.ts:12`). **No `tool-*` package declares approval rows, and `tool-automation` needs none**: the permission plugin's config selects *sandbox mode + approval policy* bundles, not per-tool gates. `packages/interaction/permission-presets/README.md:5-9` — each preset name bundles `sandbox/mode` with `approval/policy`; `custom` is derived-only.

**Where those rows live.** `packages/bundle/agent-standard/cordis.patch.yml:30-46` inserts `approval` (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-user-approval`, policy `ask` unless `DSH_PERMISSION_MODE=danger-full-access`) and `permission` (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-permission-presets` with the `read-only` / `workspace-write` / `danger-full-access` table) in the *same insert* as the tool rows. `packages/bundle/app-chat/cordis.patch.yml` disables ~23 of agent-standard's **tool** rows (`:156-272`) so the per-session preset owns them, but leaves `approval` and `permission` untouched — they stay host-plane.

**What a tool returns.** `packages/core/tools/src/index.ts:556-580`:
```ts
ToolExecutionSuccess { isError: false; value: JsonValue; content: ContentBlock[]; meta?; additionalContexts?; concludesTurn? }
ToolExecutionFailure { isError: true;  error: ToolFailure;  content: ContentBlock[]; meta?; additionalContexts? }
```
`execute` returns **only** the canonical JSON value declared by `output.schema`; the registry snapshots it as lossless JSON (`:544-553`), validates, freezes, and hands it to `output.render(args, value)`. Throwing, or returning a value that fails the schema/renderer/metadata projector, becomes `isError` (`docs/cookbook/adding-a-tool.md:46`). Wire form (from the recorded transcript): `{"type":"tool-result","toolCallId":"…","content":[{"type":"text","text":"…"}],"isError":false}`.

**Bounded-output conventions.** Three layers exist and a Notes/desktop tool should pick from them: (a) tool-owned Config caps applied to the emitted array (tool-automation `maxListed*`); (b) `finalizeContent` byte trimming against a deployment limit — `packages/jobs/tool-jobs/src/index.ts:185,233-256` caches `visibleOutputLimit(ctx, exec)` in a `tools/pre-execute` prepend listener and trims in `finalizeContent`; (c) the optional `ctx.spillStore` backend for capped results with a follow-up-readable locator (tool-fs-search). `packages/CLAUDE.md`: "Apply bounds to the complete result… test tiny and exact limits, oversized single chunks, and multibyte byte limits."

## 3. The keyless snapshot anatomy for a tool package

Five files plus one test block:

1. **`examples/headless-agent/automation.cordis.yml`** — the *runnable* overlay. `cordis-plugin-include` over `./cordis.yml` with one `insert` list: `storage`, `storage-json` (root `./.storage`), `storage-domain` (backend json), `agent-default-model` (needed because a `start` rule has no session-specific model selection), `automation-domain`, `tool-automation` (`:29`). **Yes — the example lane must mount the tool's backing service.** A `notes.cordis.yml` mirrors this with `storage*` + `note-domain` + `tool-note`; a desktop one with `desktop-domain` + `tool-desktop` (and, for a launchable Surface, a `SurfaceDefinition` row — `surface-chat` is the precedent, `packages/bundle/app-chat/cordis.patch.yml:41-44`).

2. **`examples/headless-agent/automation.cordis.snapshot.yml`** — the replay twin. It re-includes `./cordis.yml` directly (header comment `:1-4`: "a config patch cannot target an entry behind a nested include"), disables `llm-deepseek` (`:8-10`), restates the whole overlay, and appends `llm-replay` (`:33-34`) plus the fence plugin as a top-level row (`:39-40`, `name: './tests/fixtures/automation-dispatch-fence.ts'`).

3. **`examples/headless-agent/tests/snapshots/automation-rule/`** — four files, **no `session.jsonl`**:
   - `input.json` — `{ "steps": [{ "op": "prompt", "text": "…" }] }`; read by `scenarioPrompt()` (`headless.snapshot.ts:207-214`) so the spec and fixture cannot drift.
   - `replay.override.json` — a **bare `ReplayEntry[]`**, i.e. a whole-script replacement, so `deriveScriptFromFile` is never reached and the missing `session.jsonl` is fine (`packages/test-support/llm-replay/src/index.ts:509-531`). Three entries, one per model call: (1) `block-start`/`tool-call-delta`/`block-end` for `automation_create_rule` with the full JSON argument string, `usage`, `finish {kind:'tool-calls'}`; (2) the same shape for `todo_write`; (3) a text block `"AUTOMATION RULE READY"` with `finish {kind:'stop'}`. **This is how a replayed model emits a tool call** — the `tool-call` block's `id` (`call_automation_create`) is what assertions correlate on.
   - `session.1.jsonl` — the **child** script for the session the rule *starts*, supplied via `DSH_SNAPSHOT_CHILD_FILES` (the primary sidecar replaces only the primary script).
   - `stream-json.expected.jsonl` — the pinned transcript: every `session_event` record plus a terminal `result` record, normalized with `{{sessionId}}`/`{{cwd}}`/`{{system}}`/`{{tools}}` tokens. This is where the model-visible result text is pinned verbatim (`"Created automation rule rule-{{sessionId}}: \"review finished todo work\" acts on tools/result and is on now (revision 0)."`).

4. **The block in `examples/headless-agent/tests/headless.snapshot.ts:810-899`** — `it('starts an automation session from a matched tool result')`. Consts at `:35-36`. It calls `runLoaderSmoke({ label, tempDirPrefix, binScript, libBinScript, configPath, binArgs: [configPath, prompt], tsconfigPath, env: { DSH_SNAPSHOT: 'replay', DSH_SNAPSHOT_FILE, DSH_SNAPSHOT_OVERRIDE, DSH_SNAPSHOT_CHILD_FILES, NODE_OPTIONS }, prepare, inspect })`. The `inspect` callback (`:833-893`) reads persisted logs and asserts: both sessions persisted with the same cwd (`:834-841`); the ordered `tool/call` names `['automation_create_rule','todo_write']` (`:844-846`); the create result located by `message.source.callId` (`:847-856`); the started session's trigger `user/message` source `{ kind: 'plugin', plugin: 'automation', form: 'notice' }` and its text shape (`:858-865`); and the **durable domain file** `.storage/automation.json` — rule fields, owner `{ kind:'agent', sessionId: prompted.header.id }`, and one run with `outcome: 'completed'` (`:870-891`). Finally `:895-899` normalizes stdout and compares against `stream-json.expected.jsonl`, writing it back under `DSH_SNAPSHOT=refresh`.

5. **The fence fixture** — `examples/headless-agent/tests/fixtures/automation-dispatch-fence.ts`. Loader plugin, `inject = ['automations']`, registered in `knip.json:42` as an entry. It listens on `tools/result` to capture the triggering agent id, on `domain/changed` to detect a non-`started` run outcome, and wraps `agent/pre-step` to hold the triggering agent until the caused run settles. Its JSDoc is explicit that it pins **process lifetime, not dispatch behavior**. A Notes/desktop scenario whose tool call is synchronous within one turn needs no fence; one that starts a second agent does.

Runner: `pnpm run test:snapshot` (`vitest.snapshot.config.ts:47-54` includes `examples/*/tests/**/*.snapshot.ts`), refresh with `test:snapshot:refresh`.

## 4. Driving a REAL tool call through the web UI replay lane

`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/apps/web/tests/cordis-tool-round.e2e.ts` (185 lines) is the model-authored probe. Anatomy:

- **Fixture**: `apps/web/tests/snapshots/cordis-tool-round/session.jsonl` + `ui.expected.md` (`:23-24`). Unlike the headless lane this is a **real recorded session log**, not an override sidecar; `installLlmReplay` derives the script from it (`scaffold.ts:631-641`).
- **Scaffold**: `launchWebScaffold({ cordisTools: true, ...(MODE === 'record' ? {} : { replayFixture: FIXTURE, paceMs: 15 }) })` (`:74-77`). `cordisTools: true` is a bespoke patch at `scaffold.ts:525-529` inserting `{ id: 'tool-cordis', name: '@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-cordis' }` into the shipped tree — a Phase-4 e2e needs the equivalent flag (or an `extraOverlayPath` overlay, `scaffold.ts:212-217`, the pattern used by `goal-bar.overlay.yml` / `desktop-recovery.overlay.yml`).
- **Fixture↔script coupling**: `:95` asserts `fixtureUserPrompts(await readFile(FIXTURE,'utf8'))` equals the exact prompts the spec types, so the drive script cannot drift from the recording (`scaffold.ts:762-768`).
- **Drive**: fill the `textarea`, press Enter, await `scaffold.whenTurnSettled()` (`:97-101`), which resolves on the in-process `turn/end` after `ctx.sessions.flush(session)` (`scaffold.ts:676-695`).
- **Real execution under replay**: `:1-10` states the contract — *the fixture pins only what the MODEL said; the tools execute for real*. The approval is the test's own click (`:105-115`), and the DOM probe `[data-snapshot-probe]` is asserted absent before and present after (`:110`, `:116`), proving the side effect really happened. This is exactly the lever a Phase-4 e2e needs: the Notes window can be asserted to show the **committed revision** the tool actually wrote.
- **Durable-log assertions**: `assertCompleteCordisLifecycle` (`:45-64`) filters the harvested `SessionEvent[]` (collected via `scaffold.ctx.on('session/event', …)` at `:78`) for `turn/end` reason `{kind:'completed'}`, the exact ordered `tool/call` names, and one non-`isError` `tool/result` per `callId`.
- **UI-tool card assertions** (`:137-167`): rows are located by `page.locator('[data-tool="&lt;tool_name&gt;"]')` — emitted by `packages/client/ui-tool/src/client/tool/components/ToolRow.tsx:195` as `data-variant` / `data-tool` / `data-state`. A tool with **no** keyed toolview renders the generic card whose accessible name is `Tool call &lt;name&gt; · &lt;args&gt;` (see `ui.expected.md` line for `cordis_inspect_self`); a package that registers a keyed toolview replaces it (`:145-148`). `data-state` is asserted `'ok'` at `:164`. The whole row is the expand toggle: `row.locator('[aria-expanded]').first().click()` (`:152`).
- **Aria golden**: `captureStableAria(page, '[class*="centerCol"]', scaffold.workspaceCwd)` + `compareOrRefreshGolden(UI_EXPECTED, snapshot, MODE)` (`:169-179`).
- **Cleanliness**: `watchConsole(page)` tripwire asserting zero `pageErrors`/`warnings` (`:181-184`); teardown asserts the replay fixture was fully consumed (`scaffold.ts:702-708`).

Config: `vitest.web.config.ts:26-27` includes `apps/web/tests/**/*.e2e.ts`; run with `pnpm run test:web` (builds first) or `test:web:refresh`. Window-level helpers for the desktop half live in `apps/web/tests/desktop-windows.ts` (`WINDOW`, `TASKBAR`, `openWindowFromLauncher`, `desktopRecord(scaffold, page)` returning the host `DesktopSnapshot`, `boundSessions`).

## 5. Tool naming conventions and multi-action modeling

**Verb placement.** Two families coexist; the newer, subsystem-owned packages use `&lt;singular_subsystem&gt;_&lt;verb&gt;`:

- `automation_create_rule`, `automation_list`, `automation_set_enabled`
- `schedule_create`, `schedule_delete`, `schedule_list`
- `terminal_open`, `terminal_close`, `terminal_list`, `terminal_read`, `terminal_send`, `terminal_signal`
- `job_kill`, `job_list`, `job_output` — **singular** `job_`, though the package is `tool-jobs` and the service is `ctx.jobs`
- `session_search`, `session_trace`, `session_event_read`, `session_event_search`, `session_event_trace`
- `team_task_create`, `team_task_get`, `team_task_list`, `team_task_update`
- `cordis_define`, `cordis_undefine`, `cordis_run`, `cordis_stop`, `cordis_inspect_self`, `cordis_inspect_list`, `cordis_inspect_query`
- `todo_write`, `web_search`, `web_fetch`, `read_image`

The older verb-first names are `create_goal`, `get_goal`, `update_goal`, `list_agents`, `send_message`, `interrupt_agent`, `wait_agent`, `spawn_teammate`, `exit_plan_mode`, `ask_user_question`, `run_code`. Bare single-tool names carry no prefix: `bash`, `pwsh`, `read`, `write`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, `lsp`, `skill`, `subagent`, `workflow`, `ralph`, `report`, `str_replace_editor`. **Plural prefixes are never used.** Full inventory: `docs/tool-catalog.md:18-44`.

**Parameter case.** Top-level model-facing parameters are `snake_case` (`rule_id`, `include_runs`, `kill_switch`, `run_in_background`, `file_path`, `session_id`), while nested objects that mirror a durable record keep the record's `camelCase` (`target.presetId`, `target.sessionId`, `budget.maxTurns`, `rateLimit.maxPerMinute`, `causal.agentEvents`) — `packages/automation/tool-automation/README.md:9` says this is deliberate: "mirrors the `AutomationRuleInput` record field-for-field (camelCase), so a zod rejection quotes the exact parameter path the model supplied."

**Multi-action modeling — both patterns ship, chosen by whether the actions share a subject and argument shape.**
- *Single tool + action enum*: `lsp` with `operation: enum['goToDefinition','findReferences','goToImplementation','hover']` over one identical `(file_path, line, character)` cursor (`docs/tool-catalog.md:1166+`); `str_replace_editor` with `command: enum['view','create','str_replace','insert']` over one `path` (`:563+`); `cordis_inspect_*` splits into three tools but the *scope* is the discriminator, not an enum.
- *Many tools*: `terminal_*` (six, distinct arguments and lifecycles), `job_*` (three), `schedule_*` (three), `automation_*` (three).
- *Hybrid — one tool, a boolean discriminator, and a `oneOf` output*: `automation_set_enabled(enabled, rule_id? | kill_switch?)`, whose cross-field rule is enforced inside `execute` (`src/index.ts:561-570`) and whose output is a two-arm `oneOf` discriminated by a `const` `scope` (`:529-559`). This is the closest precedent for "one desktop tool that minimizes/restores/closes" versus separate tools.

## 6. Where tool rows are composed

There are **three** composition points, and which one applies depends on whether the agent is process-wide or per-session:

1. **`packages/bundle/agent-standard/cordis.patch.yml`** — one `- insert:` list carrying the standard agent's model-facing tool rows *plus* `approval` and `permission` (`:30-46`). This is what the CLI/headless/ACP profiles get. Header comment `:1-13`: "A surface that composes its agent per session (the Web bundles) **disables these rows from a later layer rather than dropping this bundle**… A patch replaces the targeted row's whole `config`, so each row states every key it owns."

2. **`packages/bundle/app-chat/cordis.patch.yml`** — the Web layer. It disables agent-standard's tool rows one by one (`:156-272`) with a comment per row explaining why the *service* stays host-plane while only the *tool* moves, then inserts `agent-presets` with `default: standard` (`:285-289`) and patches `desktop-domain` with `initialSurface: chat` (`:291-295`). The `desktop-domain` / `desktop-runtime` / `ui-desktop` rows themselves come from `packages/bundle/desktop-shell/cordis.patch.yml:15-23`, which deliberately names **no** surface.

3. **`apps/cli/config/agent-presets/standard/agent.cordis.yml`** — the roster mechanics. Header `:1-19`: the file is an **agent-plane** composition mounted **once** under a standing scope; every session naming it joins by scope parentage. The hard rule: *"A service row here MUST sit inside a group carrying an `isolate` realm"* — otherwise it publishes into the process-global root realm and `dsh-agent-presets` rejects it at mount. `isolate: &lt;key&gt;: true` means an entry-local realm.

**How a tool row references its backing service** — it does not, by name. It declares `inject` and resolves whatever `ctx.&lt;key&gt;` its scope sees. The preset file's comments encode the resulting placement rule, repeated per subsystem:
- Registry/service on the **host plane**, tool row in the preset: `tool-jobs` (`:75-76`, "the task REGISTRY stays on the host plane… an entry-local realm here is invisible to every sibling row"), `tool-goal` (`:95-96`, "the Gateway serves the goal domain as Remote endpoints whose receiver comes from a generated descriptor, so it resolves `goals` on the host"), `tool-skill`, `tool-web`, `tool-subagent*`.
- Provider **and** consumers together inside one `isolate` group when nothing outside the agent reads the service: `planning` (`isolate: planMode`), `compaction` (`isolate: compaction, toolResultPruner`), `delegation` (`isolate: workflowEngine`).

**Where desktop/notes tool rows belong.** `ctx.desktop` is a `TypertRemoteService` served to the browser as Remote endpoints `desktop/resolve|listSurfaces|openWindow|closeWindow|updateWindow|focusWindow` (`packages/desktop/desktop/README.md`), mounted by `desktop-shell` — exactly the `tool-goal` / Gateway situation. `ctx.notes` will be the same (`note-domain` over `storage-domain`, read by `ui-note` through its own Remote contribution — proposal `:190`). So the **service** rows stay host-plane in `desktop-shell` / `app-notes`, and `tool-desktop` / `tool-note` are rows in `apps/cli/config/agent-presets/standard/agent.cordis.yml` outside any `isolate` group (they publish nothing; they only register into the host `tools` registry, which is the stated criterion for `tool-fs` at `:53-55`). If they were also to serve the CLI profile, the mirror rows go in `packages/bundle/agent-standard/cordis.patch.yml` and get `disabled: true` in `app-chat`'s list.

**Where automation's tool rows WOULD go — they are nowhere in a shipped profile today.** The only composition of `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-automation` in the repository is the opt-in headless example (`examples/headless-agent/automation.cordis.yml:29` and its snapshot twin `:32`). It appears in no bundle patch, no preset, no `apps/*` config; `docs/tool-catalog.md:40` lists no shipped alias, and `packages/automation/README.md` gives no composition guidance. Per its own docs the tool row would follow the `tool-goal` shape — `automation-domain` (which needs `storage-domain`) host-plane, `tool-automation` in the preset — but that decision has not been taken.

**One caveat worth flagging for Phase 4**: `apps/cli/config/agent-presets/*/agent.cordis.yml` is mounted per-session by the roster, and `apps/web/tests/scaffold.ts:376-380` notes that a preset subtree is "a subtree this lane's include patches cannot reach, because the roster mounts it directly per session rather than as a row of the booted tree." A web e2e that needs a new preset tool row must use the `agentPresets: { roots, default }` scaffold option (`scaffold.ts:290-303`, patched in at `:517-521`) pointing at a fixture preset root, not an `extraOverlayPath` patch.</result>

## Decisions taken where the spec left room

1. **`'empty-update'` is not a `NoteMutationCode`.** My brief said "parse-level refusal like desktop's empty-set", which is what I built (a `.refine()` on `updateNoteRequest`). A code nothing can produce would be dead, so I dropped it from the union and stated the governing rule instead: fixed contract bounds (title ≤512, an update naming no field) are refused at validation; deployment bounds (`maxNotes`, `maxBodyBytes`) are refused as result values. **N2 should not expect a `'empty-update'` rejection** — `ctx.notes.update` throws on an empty `set`, so `note_update` must require at least one field in its own schema.
2. **Delete has no idempotency-replay check at all**, not just no replayable record. Any record still present whose `lastMutationId` matches is one this delete did *not* remove; answering `committed` for it would announce a deletion that never happened and break the `note/deleted` invariant. Documented in the method JSDoc, the provider, both READMEs, and the fixture.
3. **`lastMutationId` is `string`, never `null`** (unlike `DesktopSnapshot`): every create carries a mutation id, so a nullable would be a state nothing produces — and it is what makes client-chosen-id create idempotent.
4. **List ordering ties break by id** (`localeCompare`, the repo's existing idiom at `agent-presets/discovery.ts:168`), in note-domain, desktop-domain's `listDesktops`, and the fixture. Sort stability alone does not survive a restart, since the tie order would then be the medium's serialization order.
5. **Create checks in the order: identity → body size → retention bound**, mirroring desktop's request-validity-before-deployment-bound order.
6. **`packages/note/note-domain/src/index.ts` carries two `jscpd:ignore` regions** with a written justification: the per-key operation tail and the admission-drain-close teardown are the same discipline `desktop-domain` implements. Extracting a shared owner into `dsh-storage-domain` is the right eventual move but touches a committed phase-2 package beyond N1's scope — **flagging it for N5.** With those markers, `pnpm run duplication` is back to its pre-existing single clone (`client/ui-renderer/src/client/scoped-slots.tsx`, untouched by me).

| tool | parameters | notes |

**Desktop-scenario route taken — no compromise was needed.** Both premises in the spec's fallback ladder turned out false:

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Sanity check passed: 113 specs across the three packages, 100% per-file coverage with the CI gate armed, full-repo `typecheck` and `lint` both exit 0, and I re-read the ask to confirm scope (surface-note + ui-note + TestNotes, nothing in `tool-*`/`examples`/`gen-tool-catalog`).

## The face as shipped

`ctx.notes` (`packages/client/ui-note/src/client/contract/notes.ts`) — `state: SnapshotStore&lt;NotesViewState{phase,summaries}&gt;`, `note(id): ObservableSnapshot&lt;NoteDetailState&gt;`, `create(init?)`, `update(id, expectedRevision, set)`, `remove(id, expectedRevision)`, `refresh()`.

Four firmings of the spec'd face, each deliberate:

- **`NoteDetailState` is a discriminated union** (`{phase:'loading'} | {phase:'ready';record} | {phase:'missing'}`), not `{phase, record?}`. With `record?` every reader needs a narrowing check that nothing can make fail — unreachable branches that the 100% per-file gate cannot pass.
- **Outcomes alias the Host unions**: `NoteWriteOutcome = NoteMutationResult | NoteUnreachable`, `NoteDeleteOutcome = NoteDeletionResult | NoteUnreachable`. They cannot drift from the seam, and the one added arm is honest — a carrier failure reached *no* Host verdict, so the caller says "not saved" rather than naming a refusal the Host never made.
- **`create` returns the full outcome**, not `NoteRecord | undefined`. Collapsing `note-limit` into `undefined` means pressing New note does nothing with no explanation; the list pane now shows the Host's own message.
- **`expectedRevision` is the caller's and is never re-derived at send time.** This is the crux: re-reading the held revision when the write leaves *is* the silent overwrite the no-retry rule exists to prevent. The chain still serializes writes per Note so answers install in order. Double-click protection lives where it belongs — the editor disables Save while one is in flight.

Conflicts are installed (so the editor can show what it is up against) and returned unretried. Live: `note/changed` re-reads the list always and the Note only when held-and-behind; `note/deleted` settles a held cell without a read; `connection/reset` → `reload()` re-reads the list and every held cell.

**Detail cells are marked `missing`, never pruned** — an open editor is subscribed to that exact store, and a Note recreated under the same id must be able to come back. Install ordering is guarded by a per-cell generation counter rather than revision comparison, because a deleted Note has no revision and a recreated one restarts at 0. Growth bound documented honestly in the README.

## UI structure

`NoteSurface.tsx` reads `launchState.noteId` and renders either `NoteList.tsx` (rows as a semantic `&lt;ul&gt;` of buttons — a listbox option cannot contain the row's own menu control) or `NoteEditor.tsx`. Row menu via ui-primitives `Menu`; delete confirm via `Modal` (not `RiskConfirmation`, which demands an acknowledgement checkbox — over-heavy for deleting your own note).

The conflict banner covers **both** ways of learning the Note moved: a save that came back `conflict`, and a Note that advanced under an unsaved draft (tracked by `baseRevision`). While it stands, plain Save is disabled — otherwise Save would carry the draft onto the very revision the banner warns about. Reload discards the draft; Overwrite resends at `banner.revision`. The per-Window draft is tagged with its Note (`subject`), so a Window switching notes can never leak one draft onto another.

Two deviations from D10's parentheticals: the bound view **replaces** the list with a header Back control rather than collapsing it to a sidebar rail (a rail duplicating a list the Back button already reaches earns nothing at v1), and the deleted-notice offers Close-window only, since Back is already in the header.

## Flags for downstream

- **`verify-client-domain-graph` exits 1** on 17 pre-existing violations in `ui-conversation`/`ui-workspace` — both untouched in this working tree, oldest at phase 1's committed `04bc0ebbcb`. Clean for `ui-note`. It is *not* on §G's known-reds list, so N5/N6 may want it triaged.
- **N4 golden hazard**: row timestamps render through `toLocaleString`. Scrub the text and read the stable `dateTime` attribute on `[data-note-updated]`.
- **test-support ⇄ ui-note** is a devDependency-only cycle (TestNotes imports `INotes`). No gate forbids it, and there is no tsconfig project-reference cycle since references only see `src`.

Config rows added: `tsconfig.base.json`, `tsconfig.client.json`, `tsconfig.host.json`, `knip.json`, two `verify-package-readme-model-experience.ts` entries. No bundle rows (N4 owns those). Everything left uncommitted.

result: Phase-4 N3 delivered — `packages/note/surface-note` and `packages/client/ui-note` (notes mirror + list/editor surface with a no-silent-overwrite conflict contract) plus a `TestNotes` double, all uncommitted, with 113 specs green at 100% per-file coverage and full-repo typecheck/lint clean.</result>

The first attempt matched `[style*="left: 104px"]` and missed: Chromium re-serializes `inset: auto` + `left`/`top` back into the `inset` shorthand. // DynamicCordisGuardEnv
allowedWindowSurfaceKeys?: ReadonlyMap&lt;string, number&gt;   // surface key -&gt; launchSchemaVersion
// DynamicCordisClientHalf (so the policy reaches the guard through the runner)
allowedWindowSurfaceKeys?: ReadonlyMap&lt;string, number&gt;
```
In `guardedSlots`, for `slot === 'desktop.window.surface'` and only when the map is present: an unlisted (or absent) key is refused before the registry is touched; a listed key has `options.meta` **forced** to `{ launchSchemaVersion }` from the map. Teaching text:
`desktop.window.surface only accepts a Surface this Plugin declared, and "wallet" is not one of them. Register under one of: "clock", "timer".` — with `a registration without a Surface key` in place of the quoted key when none was given, and `This Plugin declared no Surface, so it has no window to render into.` for an empty map. Absent map = today's behavior exactly (drafts unrestricted, `meta` untouched). The runner threads it with a conditional spread, so the draft path never sets the property.

**`allocatePriority`** moved from the per-instance `--this.nextPriority` counter to a module-level page counter in `src/client/priority.ts`. Identical output for the single engine that exists today (-1, -2, …); made page-wide deliberately, because the registry refuses a second entry at an occupied cell's exact priority, so two engines with private counters would collide.

## Deviations / judgment calls

1. **`purpose` and `label` are trimmed too**, not just `name` — the spec only names `name`; trimming parallel free-text fields keeps them symmetric and makes the digest canonical for whitespace variants.
2. **Brand factory lives in `records.ts`**, with a local `export type PluginPackageId = &lt;alias&gt;` beside `export function PluginPackageId(...)`, so one export name carries both meanings while `types.ts` stays types-only. 4. **`canonicalArtifactJson` sorts by construction**: `JSON.stringify(artifact, keys)` where `keys` is the sorted union of the three schemas' `.shape` keys. This makes "recursively sorted keys, no whitespace" exact and branch-free, and a field added to a schema cannot silently drop out of the digest. Side effect (pinned by a test): a key no schema declares is not part of identity.
5. **Non-ENOENT medium failures propagate unchanged** rather than being mapped into a code, since the frozen list has no read-failure code. Tests pin EISDIR (load) and ENOTDIR (has) passthrough.
6. **Invariant is the explained empty installer** — the store publishes no event and retains no state; its one relation (id ⇒ bytes reproduce the digest) is enforced inside `load` before any caller sees the artifact.

It produced `lib/typert.host.{js,d.ts}` and `lib/typert.remote-client.{js,d.ts,d.ts.map}`, so `./typert` and `./remote` resolve like `dsh-desktop`'s.

I did **not** need to touch either README allowlist script: the package carries structured Model Experience entries (it steers agents) and a real Known Limitations section.

## Deviations from the spec, with rationale

1. **Two serialization lanes, one tail map.** Spec §3b puts `harness.storage` writes "under the plugin's operation tail". A host half that `await`s `harness.storage.get(...)` during activation would then deadlock against the transaction activating it. I kept one `operationTails` map and one `enqueue`, keyed by lane: `plugins:&lt;id&gt;` for registry transactions, `data:&lt;id&gt;` for that plugin's storage. `BOOT_READ_HOST` pins the case.
2. **Whole transaction inside the lane.** Rollback/enable/uninstall run load → candidate start → put → run swap inside the plugin lane; a promotion's candidate still starts outside it (the page handshake requires that), and only put → swap is in the lane. This removed an unreachable "record vanished mid-transaction" branch.
3. **A first install mints its `PersonalPluginId` when the request is armed**, not at commit (spec §3c COMMIT step 1). The candidate's host half — and its namespaced storage — runs before any record exists.
4. **`promotedAt: number`**, not `number | null`; the spec's own parenthetical says entries exist only for committed packages.
5. **`committed.runId` is `PersonalPluginRunId | null` with optional `diagnostics`** on both promotion and rollback: a commit whose Surface registration is refused installs the record but produces no run. Same for `PersonalPluginEnableResult`.
6. **`PromotionTargetView.existing.currentPackageId` is nullable** — a target can be uninstalled while its promotion is armed (tested; approving then reinstalls under the same id).
7. **`rollback` refuses a disabled plugin** (`code: 'disabled'`), so "prove the previous package comes up" stays meaningful.
8. **`setEnabled` is idempotent** — an unchanged request writes nothing and publishes nothing.
9. **`uninstall` publishes `personal-plugin/changed` at `revision + 1`** so a roster reconcile learns about removals without a third event; the invariant treats a removal as the one announcement with nothing to read back.
10. **Surfaces are registered from the service fiber with disposers held on the run**, not "on the candidate's fiber" — client-only packages have no fiber, and the run must own withdrawal deterministically either way.
11. **The one-pending-per-target check runs right after target resolution**, before the collision/bounds/store work.
12. **No own "package carries neither half" check** — the package store's artifact schema refuses it as `package-rejected`.
13. **`tsconfig.base.json` gained two `paths` entries** (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-plugin-package-store/types`, `@deepseek-ai/dsh-plugin-registry/types`). Required: the Typert generator refuses a Remote boundary type imported from a package root (`PluginPackageId must be exported from a public non-root type subpath`), and `types.ts` must stay client-plane-importable.
14. **Two `jscpd:ignore` fences** in `activation.ts` (`errorDetails`, `runHandle`): `cordis-host-runner` keeps its equivalents private, so `duplication` flagged them. Recorded as a README limitation.

## Tool catalog changes
New row + section `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-plugin-development` with `plugin_inspect`, `plugin_list`, `plugin_promote`, `plugin_rollback`. Requires `ctx.tools`, `ctx.systemPrompt`, `ctx.personalPlugins`, an owning Agent for the two mutations; writes `tool/call`, `tool/result`, `personal-plugin/promotion`, `personal-plugin/changed`. The generator does **not** derive mount placement from bundle files — `note` is hand-written, so I recorded honestly: *"Not in any shipped tree yet: the `personal-plugin-runtime` bundle that mounts these tools beside the registry, the package store, and the Plugin Studio arrives with the rest of the durable plugin runtime."* **The bundle is still pending** — update that sentence when it lands.

## Deviations (with rationale)
1. **No `dynamicCordisRunner` injection.** `PersonalPluginService.requestPromotion` resolves the draft itself (`this.rootCtx.get('dynamicCordisRunner')`, index.ts:333) and returns `draft-runtime-missing`/`missing-draft`. The tool never needs the draft service.
2. **Rollback calls `rollbackForAgent(agent, pluginId)`** — the registry's in-process Agent-attributed entry point — not an optional-agent overload of `rollback`.
3. **Manifest validation is delegated.** The tool locally validates only the three id arguments it brands; `name`/`purpose`/`surfaces`/`storage_schema_version` go to the registry's zod `promotionManifestInput`, whose `invalid-manifest` refusal names the offending field. Duplicating those rules would let the two drift.
4. **`presentPluginCall` takes `kind`** (`read` for the two listings, `execute` for the two mutations), so it is not byte-identical to tool-note's `kind: 'other'` copy. It keeps the jscpd fence and the `TODO(tools)` marker, worded for this copy.
5. **`maxInspectBytes` bounds the complete emitted result**, measured on its JSON encoding, not the source alone. The record summary is never cut (small, and it is what the model acts on); the host half fills first, then the browser half. Truncation walks code points at their exact JSON-encoded cost, so a character is whole or absent.

## What was built

**`packages/plugin-runtime/personal-client-runner` (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-personal-client-runner`)** — a two-entry client package modelled on `cordis-client-runner`:

- `src/index.ts` — node half, empty `apply` so the cordis.yml row exists.
- `src/invariant.ts` — explained empty installer (the owned relation spans a page and a host process; not observable from the node plane).
- `src/client/index.ts` — the plugin: `inject = ['loader','modules','slots','remote','remote.personalPlugins']`, the `RemoteResult`-unwrapping host seam, per-code teaching errors for `invoke` (a `Record` keyed by refusal code rather than an if-chain, so every arm is total and covered), the `ctx.personalPluginRunner` face declaration, and the event wiring (`personal-plugin/changed` → roster pull, `personal-plugin/promotion` → promotions pull, `connection/reset` and activation → both). Every verb reads `ctx.remote.personalPlugins` at call time; no client `timer` service is provided.
- `src/client/runner.ts` — one `DynamicCordisPackageRunner` with `modulePrefix: 'personal'`, the reconcile loop, and the approval orchestration.
- `tests/bench.client.ts` + `roster|routing|promotion.client.spec.ts` — 38 jsdom specs over a real `SlotRegistry`, real cordis fibers behind a stand-in loader, and a fake `personalPlugins` namespace.
- README trio, pairing recorded via `verify-translation-pairing --write`.

## Key design decisions

- **Mounts are keyed by `PersonalPluginId`, taken from `getClientCode`'s answer** (`PersonalPluginClientSource.pluginId`). The registry mints the id when it *arms* a request — even for a first install — so a candidate mounts under the id it will commit under and the post-commit reconcile is a no-op for both new installs and updates. No requestId-keyed provisional mounts, no remount after commit (both paths tested).
- **Convergence is by activation, serialized on one lane** (`lane.then(op)`), mirroring the engine's own per-package queue one level up: a roster pull arriving mid-approval runs *after* it, so no reconcile can unload an unsettled candidate. Public entry points enqueue; internal steps call `reconcile`/`pullPromotions` directly (no self-deadlock).
- **Failure unwinding rides convergence**: after `resolvePromotion(ok:false)` (or a non-committed answer to `ok:true`) the final reconcile sees a runId mismatch and re-mounts whatever the host still runs.
- **Brand seam**: the shared engine keys by the draft runtime's branded ids. The five crossings take plain `string` (a legal narrowing, no double assertion) and sit together in one commented block.
- Deviations worth naming: (1) `surfaceId` on a render report is the crashed entry's **slot**, because the supervision seam carries no registration key — documented in Known Limitations; (2) a roster-path mount failure has no host carrier (the registry only records client failures for an approval it awaits) — logged page-locally, retried on the next pull, documented.

## Files touched outside the package

`tsconfig.client.json` (project reference) and `tsconfig.base.json` (one `@deepseek-ai/dsh-personal-client-runner/client` paths entry — needed because only `packages/client/*` has a `/client` wildcard; the root and `/invariant` are covered by the existing `plugin-runtime/*` wildcards). No `vitest.config.ts` change was needed — the package reaches per-file 100% with no exclusion. No `scripts/verify-package-readme-model-experience.ts` allowlist entry either: the package authors genuinely model-visible text (the candidate-load message the registry steers to the asking Agent, and the render-failure text `plugin_inspect` shows), so the README carries two full structured Model Experience entries, matching `cordis-client-runner`'s classification.

- Scoped coverage with `--coverage.thresholds.100`: **Statements 100% (161/161), Branches 100% (35/35), Functions 100% (72/72), Lines 100% (140/140)**.

`hygiene`: `vendor rescope` (knip.json + vendoring cookbook zh) and `constraints` (`tool-plugin-development` `files` field). `doc-sync`: `verify-md-links` + `verify-translation-pairing` (only `surface-plugin-studio`'s links to the not-yet-created `ui-plugin-studio`), `gen-doc-graphs --check` and `gen-cordis-catalog --check` (unclassified `PersonalPluginId`/`PromotionRequestId` in the registry's event JSDoc — needs `linkedTypePages` entries + the new subsystem page), `gen-config-catalog --check` (store/registry Config rows absent). `duplication` also flags one pre-existing tsx clone in `client/ui-renderer/src/client/scoped-slots.tsx` (file untouched on this branch).

`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/apps/web/tests/personal-plugin-lifecycle.e2e.ts` (+ `snapshots/personal-plugin-lifecycle/approval.expected.md`). Two Hosts over one `harnessHome`/`storageRoot` under a shared mkdtemp; keyless, no replay fixture.

1. **Install** — seed v1 → assert roster empty → notice (`data-studio-notice="1"`) → open Studio → approval row shows purpose, manifest facts and host source (aria golden) → Approve → roster commits `{name:'Hello', enabled:true, revision:0, runStatus:'running'}` → open `Hello` from the launcher → `[data-hello-body="v1"]` renders `Hello from v1`.
2. **Restart** — second Host over the same roots, no seeding, reopened via `?desktop=&lt;id&gt;`; the Window redraws v1, `dynamicCordisRunner.listPlugins(...)` is `[]` (no draft in this process), launcher still lists `Hello`.
3. **Update + rollback** — seed v2 targeting the installed id → notice → Studio → row reads `New version of Hello` → Approve → the already-open Window converges to `v2` (revision 1) with no reload → `[data-studio-rollback]` → `[data-studio-confirm="rollback"]` → `[data-studio-confirm-action="rollback"]` → back to `v1` (revision 2), `listPromotions()` empty, and the chat Window's element handle still reports `isConnected` (never remounted).

**Seeding mechanism — deviation from the brief, with rationale.** I did **not** use an overlay yml. The web scaffold's Loader `baseUrl` is the temp profile dir under the harness home, and plugin names resolve through Node's internal loader against the healed `profiles/node_modules` symlink farm — so a `name: './tests/fixtures/x.ts'` row cannot resolve (wrong base) and an absolute `.ts` path would be handed to plain Node with no TS hook (vitest's transform never sees it; there is no tsx loader in `vitestExecArgv`). The precedent that *does* hold in this lane is `notes-window.e2e.ts` driving the Host service directly from the spec ("a write no browser made at all"). So `seedPromotion()` calls the real `ctx.dynamicCordisRunner.define(...)` and the real `ctx.personalPlugins.requestPromotion(...)` in-process, fully typed, before the page loads.

```
- `packages/plugin-runtime/plugin-registry/src/index.ts:447` — `agentSessionId: agent.id` (the only read in `requestPromotion`)
- `packages/extensions/cordis-host-runner/src/index.ts:1241` — `return plugin?.sessionId === agent.id` (the only read in `inspectPackage`'s `owned()`)
- `plugin-registry/src/index.ts:1186-1189` — the outcome steer goes through `ctx.get('agents')?.get(sessionId)`, which answers `undefined` for a Session no gateway created, so a stand-in delivers nothing and swallows nothing.

**serialFiles:** not registered. The lane's state is entirely inside its own `mkdtemp` (harness home, storage root, package store all follow it because `dshHomePath` resolves `DSH_HOME` after the scaffold sets it and before the bundle patches are parsed). It shares no repo or process state, exactly like `desktop-recovery`, which is also not serial.

## 3. Headless scenario — complete, not deferred

Investigating the harness changed the answer: `examples/headless-agent/tests/snapshots/desktop-tools/` has **no `session.jsonl`** — `loadReplayScript` takes a whole-script array `replay.override.json` and never reads the fixture. Goldens (`stream-json.expected.jsonl`) are written by `DSH_SNAPSHOT=refresh`, which is keyless. So the scenario is authored from hand-written scripted chunks (the documented override mechanism, phase-4's own precedent) — no recorded model output is fabricated, and there is no `.skip`.

Files: `examples/headless-agent/plugin-promotion.cordis.yml` (runnable, real model), `plugin-promotion.cordis.snapshot.yml` (replay twin), `tests/fixtures/approve-promotions.ts` (approves armed promotions on `agent/pre-step`, the `seed-desktop.ts` shape — a headless process draws no Studio Window), and two scenario dirs `snapshots/plugin-promotion{,-restart}/`.

It is a genuine restart: run 1 drafts host-only code → `plugin_promote` → fixture approves → the commit steers the approval message into the session → `plugin_list`; its `inspect` hook asserts `.storage/personal_plugin.json` and copies `.storage` + `.personal-plugins` out; run 2 is a fresh process whose `prepare` copies them back and whose only call is `plugin_list`, returning the same id **and the same digest**, `running`. A local `normalizePersonalPluginIds` tokenizes the two randomly minted ids (`greet-&lt;8hex&gt;`, `promo-&lt;8hex&gt;`) the way `normalizeGoalTimestamps` handles goal stamps; the `sha256:` package digest is deliberately left literal because it is the content address of exactly the bytes the script wrote.

`personal-client-runner/src/client/runner.ts`: `mount()` now deletes the page-local `failures` entry for the plugin right after the browser half is fetched and the mount is recorded — the engine tears the previous activation down before loading a new run id, so reconcile (via `unmount`) and an approval (which mounts a candidate straight over the installed activation) now both clear. The documented behavior (`renderFailures` JSDoc, face JSDoc, README bullet: "clears when the plugin's half is unloaded or loads again") is now true rather than aspirational.
**Test** `promotion.client.spec.ts`: "drops the crash the replaced activation left when the candidate mounts over it" — crash a registered entry, approve, assert `renderFailures` no longer holds the plugin and the candidate is loaded.

## P2 — dispose() bypassed the lane
Same file: added `private disposed` + a `private isDisposed()` accessor (read through a call so a check after an `await` is a fresh read — the direct field read tripped `typescript(no-unnecessary-condition)` because TS narrows the property across awaits). `enqueue` is now `chain` (raw lane) + `enqueue` (skips an op queued after disposal); `dispose()` sets the flag synchronously and queues the teardown on the lane. In-flight guards after each Host await: `reconcile` (post-roster), `mount` (post-`getClientCode`), `orchestrate` (post-`approvePromotion`, post-mount, so an unproven candidate is never resolved for a page that is gone — matching the README's existing "a page that goes away mid-approval leaves the request armed" contract).
**Tests** (bench gained a `held()` gate that holds any namespace answer open): roster spec "stops a pass that was waiting on the Host rather than mounting into a disposed fiber" (the requested case: blocked reconcile → dispose → release → no fetch, no mount, `removed: ['entry-1']`) and "never starts a pass queued after disposal"; promotion spec "leaves the request armed when the page is disposed while the Host decides" and "drops a candidate that arrives after the page was disposed".
README (en+zh) gained the disposal sentence.

## P3 — `surfaceId` → `slot` end to end
`plugin-registry/src/types.ts` (`PersonalPluginRenderFailure.slot`, JSDoc "The slot whose renderer threw"), the `reportRenderFailure` `@Remote` JSDoc (`@param failure - the slot, …`), the composed diagnostic message `slot "&lt;slot&gt;": &lt;message&gt;` (index.ts), the runner's report call, `lifecycle.spec.ts` (values changed to the real slot `desktop.window.surface`), `routing.client.spec.ts`, and the Studio copy (`installed.renderFailure`: `Rendering failed in {slot}: {message}` / `{slot} 渲染失败：{message}`, matching the drafts keys) plus `InstalledRow.tsx`. `plugin_inspect`'s model-facing text inherits the renamed message. ## Fixes as shipped (all in `packages/plugin-runtime/plugin-registry/`)

**F1 — disabled plugin adopted at commit.** `commitPromotion` (now a plain lane-internal method in `src/index.ts`) always writes the record; it adopts the candidate only when `next.enabled`, otherwise disposes it. The decision carries `runId: null` plus a typed `inactive: 'disabled'` field (new on the `committed` variant in `src/types.ts`), and the steered commit message gained a `disabled` branch (`promotionCommittedMessage`). Test: `promotion.spec.ts › committing one approved promotion › advances the pointer without activating a plugin switched off during the approval` — asserts commit at revision 2 with no run/surfaces, then a clean enable that registers `clock` and runs the **new** package.

**F2 + F7 — decision concurrency and admission.** All three verbs now go through `decide(requestId, transition)`: admission gate → look up the armed request → `enqueue(pluginLane(target.pluginId))` → re-read the request inside the lane. `startApproval`/`settleApproval`/`declinePromotion` are the lane bodies; `commitPromotion` no longer enqueues (it runs inside the caller's lane). A request settled by the winner answers a new `refused` status with `promotion-settled` (backed by a `settledPromotions` set, so it is distinguishable from `unknown-request`). Tests: `promotion.spec.ts › deciding one promotion while another decision is in flight › starts one candidate when two approvals of the same request race` (counts host-half starts = 1), `… commits once when two approvals of a host-only request race` (one `changed` event, `previousPackageId` still null), `… settles one way when a rejection races the resolution that commits`, `… settles one way when the resolution races the rejection that disarms`, `… refuses every decision once teardown has begun` (F7). The `previousPackageId === currentPackageId` shape is additionally guarded by a commit-time `already-current` re-check: `promotion.spec.ts › committing … › refuses a candidate a rollback made current while it was armed`.

**F3 — storageSet poisoning + facade discipline.** `putData` builds the next `PluginDataRecord` and validates it with `pluginDataRecord.safeParse` before the put, surfacing failures as a teaching error naming the plugin and key. `records.ts` replaces `z.record` for `values` with a custom schema (zod's `z.record` silently **drops** `__proto__`) that bounds keys to 1–256 chars, validates each value as JSON, and rebuilds through the new `copyStoredValues` (null-prototype container). `get()` returns a `structuredClone`; `set()` still stores a fresh host-realm JSON re-parse (equivalent isolation, and realm-safe). Tests: `units.spec.ts › the durable data record › keeps every key a plugin chose as its own data` and `… refuses a key outside its bound, a value that is not JSON, and values that are not an object`; `lifecycle.spec.ts › a plugin's own storage › refuses a key the durable record could not hold, and keeps the record readable` and `… hands out copies of what it stores, and stores copies of what it is handed`; `durability.spec.ts › replays a key named after a prototype member as the data it is` (write `__proto__`/`constructor` → restart → read back).

**F4 — commit put failure.** The `plugins.put` is wrapped; a rejection disposes the candidate, returns the request to `pending` with `commit`-phase diagnostics, and answers `failed`. Test: `promotion.spec.ts › committing … › unwinds the candidate and keeps the request when the durable write fails` (uses `pool.failNextWrites`).

**F5 — maxPlugins at commit.** Re-checked for new installs; over-bound commit answers `refused`/`plugin-limit`, disposes the candidate, request back to `pending` with diagnostics. Test: `promotion.spec.ts › committing … › refuses the second install when both were armed under a one-plugin bound`.

**F6 — uninstall vs. pending promotions.** `uninstall` now calls `dropPromotionsOf(pluginId)` first: disposes candidates, disarms, publishes `settled`, and steers the requesting Agent with a new `promotionDroppedMessage`. Approving afterwards answers `refused`/`promotion-settled`; the monotonic invariant stays strict. Tests: `lifecycle.spec.ts › uninstalling › settles the promotions of the plugin it removes, and only those` (replaces the old reinstall-under-same-id test) and `… unwinds the live candidate of a promotion whose target it removes`.

**F8 — `currentEntry` claim made true.** `personalPluginRecord` gained a `.check()` requiring `currentPackageId ∈ packages[].packageId` and `previousPackageId` null-or-∈; the defensive throw and its `v8 ignore` stay honest. Test: `units.spec.ts › the durable record › refuses a pointer naming a package the plugin does not hold`.

**F9 — uninstall data resurrection.** The queued data-lane write closure re-checks `assertLive(run)` at execution time, and `uninstall` deletes the data record **through** `dataLane(pluginId)` so it serializes behind queued writes. Test: `lifecycle.spec.ts › uninstalling › deletes the data record behind the writes its package had already queued` — new `holdWrite` harness option wraps the memory backend to hold one `data` `putRecord` open, so a second write is provably queued behind the unwind; it rejects with "storage is closed" and `storedData(...)` is `undefined`.

**F10 — candidate writes.** `LiveRun.adopted` is set in `adopt()`; `assertWritable` refuses `set`/`delete` for an unadopted run with a message naming the approval state; reads stay open. Test: `lifecycle.spec.ts › a plugin's own storage › refuses a candidate's writes until the user approves it`.

**F11 — unbounded activation.** New validated config `activationTimeoutMs` (positive int, default `DEFAULT_ACTIVATION_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000`) drives one shared helper `boundActivation` in `activation.ts`, applied inside `startCandidate` — so boot, approval, rollback, and enable are all bounded. Timeout → `host-activation` diagnostics naming the bound, best-effort `disposeRun`, caller continues. Test: `promotion.spec.ts › committing … › gives up on a host half that has not settled by the activation bound` (20 ms bound, gated `apply`; a healthy install still succeeds afterwards).

**F12 — id minting collisions.** `mintRunId`/`mintPromotionRequestId`/`mintPersonalPluginId` and `createRun` take an `IdTaken` predicate and loop via `untilFree`; the service passes `pluginIdTaken` (records + armed targets), `liveRun(...) !== undefined`, and `promotions.has || settledPromotions.has`. Test: `units.spec.ts › minting ids › mints again when the caller answers that a candidate is already taken`. **Deviation:** rather than mocking `node:crypto` (brittle — the package store also consumes randomness), the collision is injected through the predicate, which is the exact signal the loop reacts to; the service-side predicates are exercised by the ordinary suites.

**F13 — commit-flow prose.** Code order is retract → register new Surfaces → adopt as the run. Fixed the reversed clause in `README.md`/`README.zh.md` and the single sentence in `docs/subsystems/personal-plugins.md` + `.zh.md`; all three i18n sidecars re-recorded.

<result># Review: codebase grounding and technical feasibility of the plan amendment

Scope: uncommitted draft edits to `/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/.agents/notes/proposed/architecture/2026-08-26-agentic-web-desktop-repository-restructuring.md` (governing rule ~41, domain table ~55-63, new sections ~195-217, Phase 5 ~297-305, renumbered 6/8/9, verification bullet ~350, acceptance ~382-383, risks ~404-405). Every mechanism claim was checked against current branch code. Findings ranked most severe first; each is labeled **contradiction** (plan states something the code contradicts) or **silence** (plan omits machinery it will need).

## Findings

**1. (Contradiction) "Enforcement is one host check: launch resolution" — but Desktop creation seeds a Window through a second path that bypasses it.**
`DomainDesktopService.create()` calls `seedWindows()` (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/desktop/desktop-domain/src/index.ts:398-457`), which builds the `initialSurface` Window directly via `buildWindow` without going through `_openWindow` (`index.ts:303-341`), the only place the draft's preset check would live. The shipped profile sets `initialSurface: chat` (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/bundle/app-chat/cordis.patch.yml:295`). As drafted, a `shared` (Notes-only) Desktop created under the shipped config is seeded with a Chat Window at creation — exactly the "silently widening" the new acceptance bullet forbids, and it breaks the exit criterion "a second page on a `shared` Desktop operates only its allowlisted Windows" (the one-check design never refuses focus/close of an existing Window, so an out-of-preset seeded Window would be fully operable). Fix: one sentence in "Desktop presets and shared Desktops" (or the Phase 5 bullet) stating that creation-time seeding resolves through the same check — the seed is skipped, with the provider's existing warn-and-open-empty fallback (`index.ts:443-445, 452-455`), when the initial Surface's Application is outside the new Desktop's preset.

**2. (Silence) No announced signal for Application registration/withdrawal, and both promised behaviors — "appearing and disappearing with it" and "re-attempted when the Application registers" — need one.**
Evidence: the registry's activation bookkeeping is process-memory maps of `LiveRun`s (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/plugin-runtime/plugin-registry/src/index.ts:225-227`); a run is retired only via `disposeRun` driven by the registry itself (`src/activation.ts:291-299`). If an Application's desktop-minted mount fiber disposes underneath a run (Application plugin unloaded, or desktop-domain provider reload), `run.disposed` stays `false`: the roster keeps reporting the run and the storage facade keeps accepting writes (`assertLive`, `index.ts:1263-1269`). And nothing today re-attempts a `failed` activation on any external change — activation happens only at `Service.init` (`index.ts:262-271`) and explicit human transactions; the cordis "waiting" mechanism parks on missing *services* (`activation.ts:309-311`), and an Application is a desktop-registry entry, not a service, so it does not apply. The registry also does not inject `desktop` (it reads `ctx.get('desktop')` lazily, `index.ts:1188`; `static inject`, `index.ts:203`) and subscribes to no desktop events (verified by grep), so even boot ordering between registry init and app-bundle registration is unguaranteed — the re-attempt subscription is what makes it order-independent. The natural mechanism is a `desktop/applications-changed` publication, sibling of the existing `desktop/surfaces-changed` (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/desktop/desktop/src/types.ts:316-320`), with the registry retracting/marking on withdrawal and re-running activation on registration. Fix: name the event and the registry subscription in "Application identity and plugin planes"; the draft names events at this granularity elsewhere, so the omission reads as a gap rather than abstraction.

**3. (Silence) The launcher intersect needs preset contents on the wire, and the draft never says how they get there.**
"the client renders the Surface catalog intersected with the preset" and "the launcher flag gates only the launcher UI" (~213) — the client's only inputs today are the resolved `DesktopSnapshot` (deliberately record = wire value, `/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/desktop/desktop/src/types.ts:111-147`) and the `SurfaceDescriptor` catalog (`types.ts:243-260`), consumed by the launcher via `ctx.desktop.surfaces()` (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/client/ui-desktop/src/client/DesktopRoot.tsx:133`, Taskbar). Binding a preset *id* onto the snapshot lands on the wire automatically, but the allowlist and launcher flag are Host config, and today nothing projects them. The intersect requires both (a) `SurfaceDescriptor` gaining the owning Application id (implied but never stated — the draft only amends `SurfaceDefinition`) and (b) the resolve answer carrying the Desktop's effective application list plus launcher flag. Fix: one clause, e.g. "the resolved snapshot carries the preset's effective application list and launcher flag, and the Surface catalog names each Surface's Application."

**4. (Contradiction, wording) "the registry registers `personal:&lt;pluginId&gt;` as the Application … on the activation's own fiber" — a Client-only package's activation has no fiber.**
`startCandidate` returns early with `fiber: undefined` when `hostCode === undefined` (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/plugin-runtime/plugin-registry/src/index.ts:1144-1145`; `createRun`, `activation.ts:131-143`), and pure-UI Surface plugins are precisely the Client-only case. Today's manifest Surfaces are likewise not fiber effects but manual disposers on the run (`run.surfaceDisposers` filled at `index.ts:1192`, drained in `disposeRun` at `activation.ts:294`). Fix wording: "owned by the activation and withdrawn when its run is disposed" — same lifetime, mechanism that actually exists.

**5. (Silence, minor) Desktop creation on the wire is genuinely new, and the stale-preset resolve path is unstated.**
Confirmed: no create op exists today — the Remote surface is exactly `resolve`/`listSurfaces`/`openWindow`/`closeWindow`/`updateWindow`/`focusWindow` (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/desktop/desktop/src/index.ts:167-219`), and pages mint Desktops only through `resolve`'s implicit create (`/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/packages/client/desktop-runtime/src/client/service.ts:132-142`; `?desktop=`/sessionStorage identity, `identity.ts:16-39`). So "creation with an explicit preset across the shell, Remote, and `desktop_create`" is a coherent addition (new Remote op + new tool beside `desktop_list`/`desktop_open_window`/`desktop_window`), and "the default preset for page-minted Desktops" correctly covers `resolve`'s create path. Unstated edge worth one clause: a stored Desktop bound to a preset id the current config no longer declares must refuse resolve loudly rather than fall back — the acceptance bullet's "fails loud" covers it in spirit but the resolve path is where it bites. (Also note `_resolve`'s unknown-id behavior means a guest presenting a pruned shared Desktop id silently receives a fresh default-preset Desktop — current-correct, but worth remembering when phase 6 designs the guest grant.)

## Verified as sound (no change needed)

- **`registerApplication` on the desktop seam**: feasible mirror of `registerSurface` — abstract on the Service Definition (`desktop/src/index.ts:230`), lifecycle-owned effect on the calling fiber in the provider (`desktop-domain/src/index.ts:250-269`). All four registrants are enumerable and updatable together under the pre-release stance: `surface-chat/src/index.ts:38-51`, `surface-note/src/index.ts:37-52`, `surface-plugin-studio/src/index.ts` (same shape), and registry `adopt()` (`plugin-registry/src/index.ts:1184-1218`, which already catches a refusing `registerSurface`, unwinds, and records a failed activation — the exact precedent a required owning-Application check needs). The client keyed slot `desktop.window.surface` (`DesktopRoot.tsx:24`) and personal-client-runner's `allowedWindowSurfaceKeys` (`personal-client-runner/src/client/runner.ts:394`) are untouched by the Host-side addition.
- **Standing application mounts**: both claimed precedents check out — agent-presets' standing map + `PresetTree` include mounted via `ctx.plugin` (`preset/agent-presets/src/index.ts:252, 485`; `mount.ts:350`) and the registry's `personal-plugins` group fiber (`plugin-registry/src/index.ts:1364-1368`). Dependency direction is sound and acyclic: registry already imports `@deepseek-ai/dsh-desktop` and reads `ctx.get('desktop')`; desktop never imports the registry; keeping re-attempt on the registry side (finding 2) preserves that. The lifecycle hazard is real but is finding 2, not a direction problem.
- **`PersonalPluginRecord` immutable target**: the record is a one-put aggregate (`plugin-registry/src/types.ts:64-93`; per-plugin transaction lanes, `index.ts:176-186`), so an immutable target set at first promotion and refused on later change fits the existing transaction discipline; the field can schema-default to `runtime` so old records need not even force the available `personal_plugin` domain version bump (`src/spec.ts:21-28`, version 0). Failed-target diagnostics fit the closed `PersonalPluginDiagnosticPhase` union with one new member (`types.ts:114-129`).
- **One host path for the four openers** (modulo finding 1): launcher `onOpenSurface` → `DesktopRuntime.openWindow` → Remote `openWindow` → public `openWindow` → `_openWindow` (`DesktopRoot.tsx:98-103`; `service.ts:181-296`; `desktop/src/index.ts:187-189`); ui-note row action calls `ctx.desktop.openWindow` (`ui-note/src/client/index.ts:158`); `desktop_open_window` calls `ctx.desktop.openWindow` in-process (`tool-desktop/src/index.ts:473`). A new `application-not-available` member of `DesktopMutationCode` flows through both generic rejected-handlers untouched (client warn path `service.ts:250-252`; tool `committedSnapshot` throw `tool-desktop/src/index.ts:213-227`), so all callers are refused identically and the refusal is model-visible/snapshot-able.
- **Config and versioning ground**: desktop-domain has a real schemastery `Config` (`desktop-domain/src/index.ts:25-49, 207-211`) and a storage-domain version 0 to bump (`src/spec.ts:17-23`); `DesktopSnapshot` record=wire means the binding lands in resolve answers for free.
- **Overlay verification bullet**: directly supported. `desktop-recovery.e2e.ts` already boots twice against one shared `harnessHome` with a per-launch `extraOverlayPath` (`apps/web/tests/desktop-recovery.e2e.ts:86-93`), the scaffold composes bundle patches plus the extra overlay (`apps/web/tests/scaffold.ts:104-107, 414-425`), and overlays disable arbitrary composed rows by id (`desktop-recovery.overlay.yml`), so disabling an application bundle's host rows (e.g. `surface-note` from `app-notes/cordis.patch.yml:22-24`) while a previously installed app-targeted personal plugin exists in the reused home is a mechanical extension. Registry boot already contains per-plugin failure without stopping the composition (`index.ts:255-271`). The `?desktop=` override (`identity.ts:16`) plus Playwright multi-page makes the second-page shared-Desktop rehearsal real.

## Verdict

**Needs the listed changes** — specifically finding 1 (the seeding path genuinely contradicts the "one host check" claim and the acceptance bullet), findings 2 and 3 (machinery the plan will need and elsewhere names at this level of detail), and the finding 4 wording fix. Finding 5 is one optional clause. With those, the draft is well grounded: no invented APIs, every cited precedent exists where claimed, and the additions fall on existing seams (abstract service + provider, lifecycle-owned effects, one-put aggregates, schemastery config, storage-domain versions, overlay-driven e2e) that demonstrably support them.</result>
