## Review: phase-5 amendment to the agentic-web-desktop restructuring note

Scope examined: the uncommitted diff to `.agents/notes/proposed/architecture/2026-08-26-agentic-web-desktop-repository-restructuring.md` (governing rule line 41, table rows 55–59, new sections at lines 195–205 and 207–217, phase entries 297–314, verification bullet 350, acceptance bullets 382–383, risk bullets 404–405), calibrated against the implemented notes for phases 3, 4, and 7, root `AGENTS.md`, `docs/testing.md`, `docs/AGENTS.md`, `.agents/skills/dsh-prose-standard/SKILL.md`, `scripts/doc-budgets.manifest.json`, and the live tool catalog.

### Findings, ranked

**1. The installation-target contract contradicts the taxonomy paragraph: the record admits `runtime` or an Application id, but the taxonomy says agent capabilities "target `runtime` or a preset".**
Evidence: line 199 — "`PersonalPluginRecord` gains an immutable installation target — `runtime`, which is today's behavior and the default, or one Application id"; line 205 — "agent capabilities — tools, providers, agent behavior — target `runtime` or a preset". No mechanism in the text lets an installation record name a preset, and today preset rows come from preset composition files, not personal-plugin records.
Why it matters: this is the phase's central new contract, and as drafted a reader cannot tell whether preset is a deferred record target or is realized by a different mechanism entirely — exactly the ambiguity the settled routing decision ("community agent-capability plugins route to runtime/preset plane") needs the text to resolve, not restate.
Fix: state which mechanism realizes each target, e.g.: "The installation taxonomy is fixed here: agent capabilities — tools, providers, agent behavior — belong on the runtime plane or in an agent preset, and preset placement stays preset composition, never a `PersonalPluginRecord` target; application contributions target their Application, so the Chat application's community plugins are runtime-plane by default." If a preset record target is instead intended later, say "deferred" explicitly.

**2. The fifth bullet — amending the two sibling notes — is not phase-5 work and leaves settled decisions contradicted for a whole phase.**
Evidence: line 303 schedules "Amend the [multi-user runtime] and [controlled fork] notes … and retire their stale alternatives" inside phase 5, while this same amendment already records the settled rule at line 309 ("work through phase 5 lands upstream, and product-owned paths begin here"). Phase 0 (line 251) already owns "Accept or amend the related proposed Agent Notes." The bullet correctly has no exit-criterion representation, because it is not behavior.
Why it matters: from the moment this amendment lands until the phase-5 PR, two active proposed notes carry alternatives the user has already settled against — the exact condition `dsh-archive-agent-notes` exists to prevent — and the bullet pads a code phase with independent doc work, against "Choose PR history deliberately. Split independent changes."
Fix: delete the bullet from phase 5 and land the two sibling-note amendments with this amendment (same PR or an immediate doc-only companion). No exit-criterion change needed then.

**3. The amendment has no Chinese counterpart, and the recorded pairing is stale — `doc-sync` is red as drafted.**
Evidence: `git diff --stat` shows the `.zh.md` gained only 2 lines (the phase-7 "已由…实现" link); `rg "阶段"` shows the zh side still numbers 阶段 5 = auth control plane, 阶段 6 = hosted execution, 阶段 8 = collaboration; the `.i18n.yaml` records English blob `dec1b1b890` while the working file hashes to `e6d76a2cc8`, so `verify-translation-pairing` fails.
Why it matters: `docs/AGENTS.md` — "Pairs update together"; the gate will block the PR, and until translated the pair is two contradictory plans.
Fix: translate the full amendment (both new sections, the phase-5 entry, renumbered 6/8/9, the verification/acceptance/risk bullets, table rows, governing rule) into the `.zh.md`, then re-record with `pnpm run verify-translation-pairing --write`. Mechanical, but it is part of this deliverable, not of phase 5.

**4. The verification bullet omits the keyless snapshot for `desktop_create` and any browser coverage for the Studio target/export display.**
Evidence: line 350 names snapshots only for "the typed launch rejection and target-failure diagnostics". `desktop_create` is a new model-facing tool (the live catalog and `packages/desktop/tool-desktop/README.md` carry only `desktop_list`, `desktop_open_window`, `desktop_window`), and `plugin_promote` implicitly gains target/export arguments; `docs/testing.md`: "Every non-trivial model-, protocol-, or human-visible change adds or updates a keyless scenario in the same PR" and "New capability seams … name every coverage tier at plan time." The Studio display (line 300) is product-user-visible with no named lane (the GIF bullet at line 353 covers it generically; the web snapshot lane is not named).
Why it matters: the phase-4 and phase-7 precedents show these snapshots are demanded per tool round; leaving them unnamed at plan time is what the testing policy's last sentence forbids.
Fix, replacing line 350: "Desktop presets and installation targets receive keyless snapshots for `desktop_create`, the `plugin_promote` target and export round, the typed launch rejection, and target-failure diagnostics; browser e2e for a second page refused outside a `shared` Desktop's allowlist and for the Studio's target and declared-export display; and overlay coverage proving an absent Application fails its targeted plugins by name without stopping desktop boot." (No new `SessionEventMap` member is needed — the rejection reaches the model as an ordinary tool result, and desktop/preset state is deliberately non-Session state per the line-45 governing rule; the note is right to say nothing.)

**5. "the plugin-development guidance" names no real artifact; the taxonomy has three concrete homes the bullet should name.**
Evidence: line 302. The homes that exist: `docs/user/develop/` (the user-facing plugin tutorial series — `basic/publish.md` is where an author learns where a plugin installs), the model-facing pair `apps/cli/config/agent-presets/cordis/skills/cordis-plugin-development/SKILL.md` plus the `tool:plugin-development` prompt section in `packages/plugin-runtime/tool-plugin-development` (agents choose the target at `plugin_promote` time; prompt-section wording is behavior and churns snapshots), and `docs/subsystems/personal-plugins.md`, which must document the new record field regardless ("the owning subsystems page updates in the same change").
Why it matters: `docs/AGENTS.md` — machine-checkable links, never free prose; and an unnamed home invites the taxonomy landing somewhere the audience never reads.
Fix: "…and document the community-plugin taxonomy in the `cordis-plugin-development` skill and `tool:plugin-development` prompt section, [`docs/user/develop`](../../../../docs/user/develop/basic/publish.md), and the [personal-plugins subsystem page](../../../../docs/subsystems/personal-plugins.md)" — or a chosen subset, but named and linked.

**6. "Plane" is now overloaded against "control plane" in the same document, and the concept competes with its own field name.**
Evidence: "control plane"/"control-plane" appears 12 times in this note; the new text adds "plugin planes" (heading, line 195), "named planes", "target plane", "the application plane is new", "runtime-plane", "per-plane … confinement", "names its plane" (line 383) — while the durable field is named "installation target" (line 199). The phase-5 heading "application planes and desktop presets" (line 297) sits directly above "Phase 6: authenticated control plane" (line 307).
Why it matters: prose standard — one exact term per subject; "application plane" reads as a deployment tier beside the control plane, which it is not. The adjectives host-plane/preset-plane are established in the implemented notes, but the nominalized standalone "plane" is new and colliding.
Fix: use "installation target" as the concept everywhere the field is meant — retitle the section "Application identity and plugin installation targets", retitle phase 5 "application installation targets and desktop presets", and change line 383 to "names its installation target"; keep the established runtime-/preset-/host-plane adjectives where they already exist.

**7. Four exact-term/metaphor violations in the new text, one of them inside the exit criterion.**
Evidence and fixes (root `AGENTS.md`: "Do not use metaphors"; `docs/AGENTS.md`: "Name the exact check… instead of metaphorical 'gate', 'vocabulary', or 'surface'"):
- Line 305 exit criterion: "no surface added by this phase accepts a caller-supplied identity" — lowercase generic "surface" in a note where `Surface` is a branded object, and "identity" is unspecified; also not mechanically checkable as written. Replace: "and no Remote method or tool added by this phase accepts a caller-supplied user identity."
- Line 215: "a second browser page standing in for the guest" — metaphor. Replace: "with a second browser page in place of the authenticated guest phase 6 adds"; correspondingly "Rehearse sharing" (line 302) → "Exercise sharing", matching phase 7's own verb.
- Line 405: "An Application allowlist reads as privacy but is not resource narrowing" — replace with the actor and the error: "An owner can mistake an Application allowlist for resource narrowing: sharing a Desktop shares every list its Applications expose…"
- Line 213: "The launcher flag gates only the launcher UI" → "The launcher flag controls only whether the launcher renders." Minor, same rule: line 199 "mints one standing application mount" → "opens one standing application mount" ("mint" is this note's id-creation verb — "page-minted Desktops", the Window-id mint).

**8. The exit criterion covers bullets 1, 3, and 4 well but leaves bullet 2's Studio display unrepresented, and the risk bullet pins the share grant to a phase whose entry doesn't carry it.**
Evidence: line 305 never mentions the target/export display that line 300 delivers; line 405 says "the phase 6 share grant must state both before it is issued", but phase 6's bullets (309–312) contain no grant and line 217 says only "Multi-user sharing later adds… a grant".
Why it matters: precedent (phases 3/4/7) allows a single probing scenario rather than bullet-complete coverage — the doc bullets rightly have no exit representation — but the Studio display is the one user-visible deliverable of bullet 2's second half, and the phase-6 pin is an internal inconsistency.
Fix: fold the display into the existing clause — "an application-targeted personal plugin, approved with its target and declared exports shown in the Studio, runs under its Application's mount…"; and either add a guest-grant bullet to phase 6 or unpin the risk: "the share grant, when it is introduced, must state both before it is issued."

**9. (Boundary with the architecture lane, noted briefly.) The absent-preset misconfiguration case is unnamed.** The text specifies the absent-Application failure for targeted plugins (line 199) but never says what a stored Desktop does when its immutably-bound preset id is no longer in `desktop-domain` config — the acceptance bullet's "misconfiguration fails loud" (line 382) covers it only generically. One sentence in the presets section naming the behavior (fail loud at load, or refuse every launch with the Desktop still bootable and named in recovery) plus a coverage case would close it.

### Direct answers to the examined questions

- **Sizing (Q1): one coherent phase — do not split, do not reorder.** Application identity is the shared foundation both halves consume (presets allowlist Application ids; installation targets name Application ids and run under their mounts); either half alone is a thin phase with a weak exit (identity alone has no user-visible behavior; presets have no subject without identity). Machinery is at or below phase-4 scale — zero new packages versus phase 4's new package family plus tools, phase 3's window manager, or phase 7's six packages — with wide-but-shallow touches. Removing bullet 5 (finding 2) is the only scope change warranted.
- **Policy checklist (Q2):** GIF — present (note-wide bullet, line 353). Storage-domain bump — present with consequence ("rejects prior records", line 209). Agent Note in the implementing PR — repo-wide standing order, consistently not restated per phase. Generated catalogs (tool, config, persistence) — enforced by the repo-wide `doc-sync` gate; consistent with prior phases not naming them. `SessionEventMap` — genuinely none needed (see finding 4). Gaps: findings 3, 4, 5.
- **Exit criterion (Q3):** mechanical and comparable to phases 3/4/7 except the two repairs in findings 7 and 8; the taxonomy and cross-note bullets lacking exit representation is acceptable for doc work (precedent: phase 1's catalog updates), and finding 2 removes the cross-note bullet anyway.
- **Budgets (Q4):** `scripts/doc-budgets.manifest.json` lists nine standing docs only; no `.agents/notes/` path is budgeted, and `docs/AGENTS.md` says review governs unbudgeted tiers — no ceiling risk for the enlarged note.
- **Taxonomy home (Q5):** finding 5.

### Verdict

**Needs the listed changes.** Findings 1–3 block landing (1 and 2 change the plan; 3 is gate-red); 4–8 are should-fix wording and coverage repairs with exact replacements above; 9 is optional here if the architecture reviewer carries it. The phase itself is correctly scoped and ordered.</result>
